Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate you taking the time. 

Um... well, I guess I'm here because... I work as a camera operator and video editor, and, um... I've just been having a little bit of trouble at work lately. I hope it's okay to start with that? It's probably just me being a little scattered, but... what finally scared me into actually making an appointment is that I keep making these really dumb technical mistakes. Things I absolutely know better than to make. 

It's actually pretty embarrassing to admit, honestly. But, like, I'll label camera cards wrong, or I'll miss a timecode note. Just recently, I exported a client cut with the wrong lower-third graphic on it... and the worst part is I had literally just checked it. I really try to do a good job and be professional, so I just... I don't know why it keeps happening. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you can help with?

Um, well, that's a really good question. I mean, I guess I've always been a little... energetic? But as far as it being *this* disruptive, where I'm actually making these embarrassing mistakes... that's relatively new. 

I'd say it's been going on at this level for about... maybe two years and two months? Give or take? We had this big company readjustment right around then, and it completely changed how my shoots and my edit reviews are run. I really, really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or blaming my managers, because I know I should be able to adapt and handle it like everyone else does... but that's when it really started becoming a noticeable problem for me. 

I hope that makes sense? Please let me know if I'm not explaining things well, or if you need me to be more specific. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Um... well... if I'm being completely honest... I guess I've always been a little bit... extra energetic? Even way back before work, like, all the way back in elementary school. 

I'm a little embarrassed to even bring it up, because I really don't want you to think I was a bad kid or a troublemaker or anything, but... my teachers actually used to have to move my desk around a lot. I guess I was just always getting out of my seat, or I'd blurt out answers without raising my hand... or I'd accidentally bother my classmates before I even realized I was doing it. I really wasn't trying to be disruptive! I just... I kind of did things before I could stop myself, I guess. 

So, to answer your question, I suppose that kind of restlessness has always been there in the background, even if the work mistakes are newer. I hope it's okay that I went all the way back to childhood? Does that help give you a better idea of things?

just trying to keep up with me.\n- key: S016\n  name: Blurting_Answers\n  description: Often blurts out an answer before a question has been completed.\n  manifestation: I jump in with answers before the director or client has finished their question, and sometimes halfway through I realize I answered the wrong thing because I reacted to the first few words.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I catch myself cutting into other people’s conversations or leaning over an assistant’s shoulder to fix something on their timeline before they’ve asked for help, and it comes off like I don’t trust them.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even in elementary school, teachers moved my desk because I was always out of my seat, blurting answers, or bothering classmates before I realized I was doing it.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My producers have started trusting me less on complex shoots, and my chance at moving into a senior director of photography role has been slipping despite the quality of my camera work.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 years and 2 months\n  manifestation: It has been going on at this disruptive level for about two years and two months, since the company readjustment changed how my shoots and edit reviews run.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 38 / television_video_or_film_camera_operator_or_editor / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S001\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Before that company restructuring two years ago, would you say you had any trouble with concentration or focus at work, or was that really the first time these kinds of issues started?\n\nPatient: Um... well... if I'm being completely honest... I guess I've always been a little bit... extra energetic? Even way back before work, like, all the way back in elementary school. \n\nI'm a little embarrassed to even bring it up, because I really don't want you to think I was a bad kid or a troublemaker or anything, but... my teachers actually used to have to move my desk around a lot. I guess I was just always getting out of my seat, or I'd blurt out answers without raising my hand... or I'd accidentally bother my classmates before I even realized I was doing it. I really wasn't trying to be disruptive! I just... I kind of did things before I could stop myself, I guess. \n\nSo, to answer your question, I suppose that kind of restlessness has always been there in the background, even if the work mistakes are newer. And, um, I do tend to fidget a lot when I'm watching a movie with my boyfriend, or I might get up to grab a snack or check my phone a few too many times... but he's really patient with me. \n\nI wouldn't say it causes any major problems, though! I really try to keep it under control so I don't annoy anyone. I just... I like to stay active and engaged, I suppose. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is... I really do value my relationships so much.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you were in elementary school with the desk-moving and blurting out answers — do you remember if you were also having trouble keeping your mind focused on your schoolwork, or was it mainly just the fidgeting and impulsivity?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Um... well, thinking back to school... I really think it was mostly just the fidgeting and the blurting things out. I mean, I always tried really hard to do a good job on my assignments! I wanted my teachers to be happy with me, you know? 

I don't really remember having a ton of trouble actually focusing on the work itself, as long as it was something I was supposed to be doing. I think I just... I had a lot of energy, and maybe I finished things quickly and then didn't know what to do with myself, so I'd get a little squirmy. But I really don't think my mind was wandering off or anything like that. I always tried to pay attention. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm remembering it right... it was a long time ago, but I'm pretty sure the focus part wasn't really the issue back then.

Just recently, I exported a client cut with the wrong lower-third graphic on it... and the worst part is I had literally just checked it. I really try to do a good job and be professional, so I just... I don't know why it keeps happening. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these kinds of mistakes and difficulty concentrating at work — was this something that's been going on for most of your life, or is this something relatively new that started more recently?\n\nPatient: Um, well, that's a really good question. Give or take? We had this big company readjustment right around then, and it completely changed how my shoots and my edit reviews are run. I really, really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or blaming my managers, because I know I should be able to adapt and handle it like everyone else does... but that's when it really started becoming a noticeable problem for me. \n\nI hope that makes sense? Please let me know if I'm not explaining things well, or if you need me to be more specific. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two years, have you noticed your concentration problems and mistakes coming and going in waves, or has it been pretty constant and steady the whole time?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... well, it's pretty much been steady, I guess? I mean, I wouldn't say it's like a rollercoaster or anything where I have these huge dramatic swings. It's just sort of this constant, low-level... hum of me being a little scattered, if that makes sense? \n\nI really try to stay on top of it, and some days I definitely do a better job of managing it than others. I don't want it to sound like I'm completely falling apart every single day, because I'm really not! I still get my work done. It's just... yeah, the struggle to not make those silly mistakes has been pretty consistent since the restructuring. \n\nDoes that answer your question? I want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. I know it probably sounds like I'm just not trying hard enough, but I really am.\n\nDoctor: Before that company restructuring two years ago, would you say you had any trouble with concentration or focus at work, or was that really the first time these kinds of issues started?\n\nPatient: Um... well... if I'm being completely honest... I guess I've always been a little bit... extra energetic? I guess I was just always getting out of my seat, or I'd blurt out answers without raising my hand... or I'd accidentally bother my classmates before I even realized I was doing it. I really wasn't trying to be disruptive! I just... I hope it's okay that I went all the way back to childhood? Does that help give you a better idea of things?\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school and getting out of your seat or blurting things out — is that still something you experience now as an adult, or has that part gotten better over time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, the getting out of my seat part is definitely much better! I guess I still do that a bit. \n\nI really don't do it to be rude or talk over anyone! I just get so eager to help or keep the momentum going, you know? Like, when I'm in a session, I'll sometimes jump in with answers before the director or the client has even finished asking their question. And then... well, sometimes halfway through my sentence, I'll realize I'm answering the completely wrong thing, just because I reacted to the first few words they said instead of waiting for them to finish. \n\nI really try hard to catch myself and be a good listener. I just want to be as helpful and efficient as possible for them. Does that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound completely unprofessional... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question honestly.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, in your personal life or relationships, have you noticed these kinds of issues with focus, impulsivity, or restlessness causing problems for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, in my personal life? I mean, I think things are mostly fine there. But it's only because I'm so excited about what they're saying and I just want to agree with them or share a story that relates! I really hope they know I'm not trying to make it all about me. And, um, I do tend to fidget a lot when I'm watching a movie with my boyfriend, or I might get up to grab a snack or check my phone a few too many times... but he's really patient with me. \n\nI wouldn't say it causes any major problems, though! I really try to keep it under control so I don't annoy anyone. I just... I like to stay active and engaged, I suppose. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is... I really do value my relationships so much.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school with the desk-moving and blurting out answers — do you remember if you were also having trouble keeping your mind focused on your schoolwork, or was it mainly just the fidgeting and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Um... well, thinking back to school... But it wasn't because I didn't care or wasn't trying! I just... I think I just wanted to finish so I could move on to the next thing. \n\nBut overall, I think I did fine academically. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: When you were in school and doing your assignments, do you remember ever having trouble actually sitting down and getting started on homework, or did you find yourself getting distracted by other things even when you were trying to focus on studying?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I just didn't really have a big problem with getting started or staying on task, as long as I knew it was something I had to do.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school, do you remember having trouble organizing your thoughts, keeping track of your belongings, or remembering instructions that your teachers gave you — things like that?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I was always pretty good about keeping track of my things, I think. I mean, my mom helped me a lot, making sure my backpack was packed and everything, so I didn't really lose things. I wanted to be a good student, so I always tried to listen really carefully when the teacher was talking. \n\nI guess... I mean, maybe sometimes I'd forget a pencil or something small, but nothing major. And as far as organizing my thoughts... I think I just kind of did the work the way they asked us to. I didn't really feel like my brain was all over the place or anything. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, definitely not. I mean, I've always tried really, really hard to be professional and meticulous in all my jobs. Before this whole restructuring thing, I really didn't have a pattern of careless mistakes at all. I was always the one people could rely on to get the edits right, you know? 

It's really just been... well, since the company readjustment changed how my shoots and edit reviews run. That was about... two years and two months ago, I think? That's when everything got so disruptive and the mistakes started happening at this level.

I just don't want you to think I've always been struggling with this, because I really haven't. It's just been these last couple of years with all the changes. Am I explaining that okay?

I really think it was mostly just the fidgeting and the blurting things out. I mean, I always tried really hard to do a good job on my assignments! I was doing a good job, I promise! Does that make sense? I just want to make sure you know I was really dedicated to my job.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the work mistakes you've been making these past two years — the mislabeled cards, the missed timecodes, the wrong graphics — do you think those mistakes are happening because your brain is having trouble keeping track of details, or do you think they're more about the stress and changes in how the work is organized making it harder to stay on top of everything?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I really think it's just the stress and all the changes. I hope you don't think I'm just incompetent or lazy now? I really am trying so, so hard to be careful. Is it okay that I'm sharing this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture, even the parts I'm really ashamed of, so I can get back to doing a good job. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're working on these projects and you make those mistakes — like exporting with the wrong graphic or missing the timecode — do you feel like you didn't notice the error because you weren't paying close enough attention, or do you feel like you were rushing through and didn't take the time to carefully check your work before moving on?\n\nPatient: Um... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I guess if I really think about it... the rushing part isn't entirely new. I mean, the mistakes are worse now, but... the feeling of just needing to move, to go fast, to just blurt things out or get onto the next thing... I guess I've always had a bit of that energy? 

Like, even way back in elementary school, I was kind of... well, I guess you could say I was a handful. I don't want to sound like I was a bad kid or anything, I was just really eager! But, um, my teachers actually had to move my desk a lot. I was always popping out of my seat, or blurting out the answers before I was called on, or accidentally bothering the kids next to me before I even realized what I was doing. 

I really tried to be good, I promise, it was just like my motor was always running a little too fast. I've worked really hard to channel that energy into my career, which usually works great for fast-paced shoots! But... yeah, I guess that rushing, impulsive feeling has always been there in some way. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?

I guess I was just always getting out of my seat, or I'd blurt out answers without raising my hand... or I'd accidentally bother my classmates before I even realized I was doing it. I really wasn't trying to be disruptive! I just... I kind of did things before I could stop myself, I guess. \n\nSo, to answer your question, I suppose that kind of restlessness has always been there in the background, even if the work mistakes are newer. I hope it's okay that I went all the way back to childhood? Does that help give you a better idea of things?\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school and getting out of your seat or blurting things out — is that still something you experience now as an adult, or has that part gotten better over time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, the getting out of my seat part is definitely much better! I guess I still do that a bit. \n\nI really don't do it to be rude or talk over anyone! I just get so eager to help or keep the momentum going, you know? Like, when I'm in a session, I'll sometimes jump in with answers before the director or the client has even finished asking their question. And then... well, sometimes halfway through my sentence, I'll realize I'm answering the completely wrong thing, just because I reacted to the first few words they said instead of waiting for them to finish. \n\nI really try hard to catch myself and be a good listener. I just want to be as helpful and efficient as possible for them. Does that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound completely unprofessional... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question honestly.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, in your personal life or relationships, have you noticed these kinds of issues with focus, impulsivity, or restlessness causing problems for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, in my personal life? I really try to be a good friend and partner, you know? \n\nI guess... well, I might sometimes interrupt my friends when we're talking, just like at work. I really try to keep it under control so I don't annoy anyone. I just... I like to stay active and engaged, I suppose. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I really think it was mostly just the fidgeting and the blurting things out. I mean, I always tried really hard to do a good job on my assignments!
